# Rodin agenda C evidence

Generated by `cargo run -p rodin-survey`. The survey fetches crates.io sparse-index files sequentially, with User-Agent `rodin-survey/0.1.0 (https://github.com/bearcove/vx)`, and caches them under `target/rodin-survey-cache/` so repeat runs are offline. Seed crates: axum, tokio, serde, clap, reqwest, syn, rand. Closure cap: 400 crates. Actual closure size: 400 crates.

## Method

- Walk: start from the seed crate names, fetch each sparse-index file, parse every JSONL version row with `facet-json`, enqueue dependency names from `package` when present or `name` otherwise, and stop at the closure cap.
- Measurement A: ignore yanked versions for block construction, sort remaining versions by semver order, normalize each row to resolution metadata (`deps` by name/req/features/optional/default_features/target/kind plus merged `features`/`features2` maps), and count contiguous runs of identical metadata. Yanked counts are reported separately.
- Measurement B: choose the 20 closure members with the most non-yanked versions, collect real requirement strings targeting those packages from the fetched index rows, convert them through `rodin_facts::VersionSet::from_req`, and run 100 chained `Region::subtract` exclusions from a full version box plus a companion `Region::intersect` chain.

## Aggregate

| metric | value |
|---|---:|
| crates | 400 |
| non-yanked versions | 15309 |
| yanked versions | 934 |
| metadata blocks | 8139 |
| overall versions/block | 1.880944 |
| median versions/block | 1.75 |
| p90 versions/block | 5 |
| max versions/block | 26 |
| share of crates with <=5 blocks | 28.75% |

## Verdict

The data does not support the strong agenda C falsifier target, "block-ratio brutal, hundreds-to-few, for almost all packages." The measured collapse is only 1.880944 versions/block overall: median 1.75, p90 5, and just 28.75% of crates have <=5 blocks, with 15309 non-yanked versions becoming 8139 metadata blocks. The counterexamples are not edge noise: async-graphql (350 blocks), clap (208 blocks), openssl (161 blocks). What does survive is the narrower claim that metadata blocks remove some raw-version churn, and the Region subtract stress did not explode on these real-shaped one-dimensional exclusions; the worst chained subtract frontier reached 11 fragments.

## Round 2

Round 2 keeps the Round 1 closure and sparse-index cache fixed, then asks whether the counterexamples are mostly lockstep family edges or whether the useful granularity is per-edge rather than whole-manifest.

### Method

- Lockstep detection: for each unique `parent -> actual-dep` edge, scan the parent's non-yanked version sequence. A row is lockstep when its requirement parses as exactly one comparator, either exact `=x.y.z` or caret/full-triple `^x.y.z`/`x.y.z`, and that comparator version equals the parent version. An edge is classified lockstep only when every present row is lockstep and it appears in at least two parent versions.
- Per-edge blocks: for each non-lockstep `parent -> actual-dep` edge, count contiguous runs across the full non-yanked parent version sequence using only that edge's metadata (`req/features/optional/default_features/target/kind`). Absence is a state, so add/remove transitions count.
- Post-fusion whole-manifest blocks: repeat Round 1's whole-manifest block count, but drop lockstep dependency rows from the metadata identity, leaving the feature maps unchanged.

### Round 2 verdict

Family fusion finds real structure: 83 of 4513 unique edges are lockstep (1.839131% by edge count, 3.501598% by dep rows). It improves whole-manifest blocks from 8139 to 7894, and overall versions/block from 1.880944 to 1.939321, but that is still not a big collapse: fused median is 1.833333, p90 is 5.333333, and only 30.5% of crates have <=5 blocks. Per-edge granularity is much stronger: 4430 non-lockstep edges collapse to 23207 edge blocks over 310719 parent-version observations, with median 11, p90 41.666667, max 220, and 77.291196% of edges at <=5 blocks. So family fusion alone does not rescue the whole-manifest frontier bet; per-edge granularity is the only round-2 hypothesis that looks empirically useful, and it points away from whole-manifest metadata blocks as the primary volume unit.

## Round 3

Round 3 measures dynamic derivation-support widening. The core used for this round propagates version domains as sets and only picks a point version when an active package must be expanded and multiple real candidates remain; conflicts below are therefore late-discovery conflicts, not avoidable set-intersection artifacts.

Natural workloads use cached crates.io sparse-index rows with each requested root pinned to its cached latest exact version and a bounded v0 mini-closure. The pressure suite is synthetic where needed so every burial shape has an explicit late-discovery choice point.

### Round 3 verdict

Derivation-support widening pays in this round: it expands exclusion coverage by 1.111111x and the perturbed pressure re-solves save 7 decisions and 5 conflicts. The effect is not uniform by kind, so the useful design signal is the per-kind table rather than a single global rescue story.

### Appendix: downgrade-cascade full trace

This trace is emitted by the round-3 solver run for the pressure scenario, not reconstructed by hand.

1. demand client [-inf,+inf)
2. domain client@1 becomes active
3. decree tokio-like [1.0.0,1.0.1)
4. domain tokio-like@1 becomes active
5. tokio-like@1: [1.0.0,1.0.3) ∩ [1.0.0,1.0.1) = [1.0.0,1.0.1)
6. force tokio-like@1 1.0.0 (only candidate after propagation)
7. force client@1 1.0.0 (only candidate after propagation)
8. domain codec@1 becomes active
9. codec@1: [1.0.0,1.0.3) ∩ [1.0.2,1.0.3) = [1.0.2,1.0.3)
10. domain web@1 becomes active
11. force codec@1 1.0.2 (only candidate after propagation)
12. expand web@1 1.0.2 (highest remaining of 3 candidates) - reads 1 manifest
13. tokio-like@1: [1.0.0,1.0.1) ∩ [1.0.2,1.0.3) = empty -> conflict
14. learn: tokio-like@1 [1.0.0,1.0.1); web@1 [1.0.2,1.0.3) dead; support: web@1->tokio-like@1 edge; widened 1->1 versions
15. expand web@1 1.0.1 (highest remaining of 3 candidates) - reads 1 manifest
16. codec@1: [1.0.2,1.0.3) ∩ [1.0.0,1.0.1) = empty -> conflict
17. learn: codec@1 [1.0.2,1.0.3); web@1 [1.0.1,1.0.2); client@1 [1.0.0,1.0.1) dead; support: client@1->codec@1 edge, web@1->codec@1 edge; widened 1->1 versions
18. expand web@1 1.0.0 (highest remaining of 3 candidates) - reads 1 manifest
19. solution: client@1 1.0.0, codec@1 1.0.2, tokio-like@1 1.0.0, web@1 1.0.0
20. summary: manifests read 3, candidate observations 3, conflicts 2, widened versions killed 2 vs 2 point versions
## Round 4: real-world Cargo.lock evidence

Source snapshot: `/Users/amos/oss/facet-cc` Cargo.lock plus local Cargo registry cache. The extractor is offline-only, indexes cached registry alternatives for locked package names, and encodes the Cargo.lock snapshot as decrees to the representative locked version for each package name. Rodin models one selected version per package name; Cargo.lock entries with multiple coexisting versions are classified rather than hidden.

### Extraction

Locked versions use Cargo.lock's concrete dependency list as active edges. Cached alternative versions use parseable manifest dependencies, while dev, optional, and target-conditional manifest dependencies are classified as modeling gaps and left inactive in this round.

## Round 5: per-major Cargo range evidence

Source snapshot: `/Users/amos/oss/facet-cc` Cargo.lock plus local Cargo registry source/cache artifacts. The extractor is offline-only, reads local Cargo.toml files and `.crate` archives directly, indexes cached registry alternatives for locked package names, and models Cargo-compatible coexisting domains keyed by package name and compatibility class.

### Extraction

Readable manifests contribute real requirement ranges. Cargo.lock supplies the oracle snapshot and, where a dependency line names an exact locked version, a compatibility-class hint for rebinding that range into the right Rodin domain. Dev, optional, and target-conditional manifest dependencies are classified as modeling gaps and left inactive in this round.

- lock unique package names: 813
- workspace roots: 139
- indexed package names: 1247
- indexed package versions: 2262
- indexed dependency edges: 4440
- extraction/modeling gaps: 16740

## Round 6: Cargo feature activation evidence

Source snapshot: `/Users/amos/oss/facet-cc` Cargo.lock plus local Cargo registry source/cache artifacts. The extractor is offline-only, reads local Cargo.toml files and `.crate` archives directly, indexes cached registry alternatives for locked package names, models Cargo-compatible coexisting domains keyed by package name and compatibility class, and evaluates target-conditionals against `aarch64-apple-darwin`.

### Extraction

Readable manifests contribute real requirement ranges and feature tables. Cargo.lock supplies the oracle snapshot and, where a dependency line names an exact locked version, a compatibility-class hint for rebinding that range into the right Rodin domain. Feature activation uses Rodin feature atoms with resolver-v2 scopes: normal features unify, build/dev scopes do not unify with normal, build dependencies are active for the host, dev dependencies are active only for workspace members, and target-conditional edges are filtered by the fixed round target.

## Round 7: Sharpened Cargo oracle evidence

Source snapshot: `/Users/amos/oss/facet-cc` Cargo.lock plus local Cargo registry source/cache artifacts. The production extractor is offline-only, reads local Cargo.toml files and `.crate` archives directly, indexes cached registry alternatives for locked package names, and models Cargo-compatible coexisting domains keyed by package name and compatibility class. The fresh-lock oracle is test-only: it runs Cargo offline in a throwaway workspace copy and never feeds Rodin's solve path.

## Round 8: Per-row Cargo oracle convergence STOP

Round 8 started with two concrete extraction bugs from the remaining row set:

- Local workspace manifests whose package version is inherited with `version.workspace = true` were not indexed as local manifests, which made `rust-examples@0.10`, `wasm-browser-tests@0.10`, and `wasm-inprocess-tests@0.10` appear as `ManifestMissing`.
- Member dependencies written as `foo.workspace = true` or `{ workspace = true, ... }` were not resolved through the root `[workspace.dependencies]` table before Rodin built dependency edges, so many real workspace requirements were either absent or degraded to wildcard requirements.

Both extraction bugs are fixed in the extractor. The parser now:

- indexes local manifests by package name without requiring a literal package version;
- parses root workspace dependency definitions once;
- resolves inherited workspace dependencies before dep-kind, target, feature, and semver filtering;
- rejects unresolved inherited workspace dependencies as `WorkspaceDependencyUnresolved` extraction gaps rather than solving them as unconstrained `*` requirements;
- emits typed per-row verdict fields in the facet-json report shape: `ExtractionBug`, `SemverModelInterpretationDifference`, or `LegitimateTieBreakDifference`, plus an optional accepted-divergence policy name.

The full Round 8 matrix did not complete after those extraction fixes. The fixed-target release evidence path completed extraction and solve with the faithful graph:

| projection | indexed edges | extraction/modeling gaps | solve wall ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| fixed target/runtime | 8280 | 8737 | 2736 |

The lock-union/dev projection completed extraction but did not complete solve before this round was stopped:

| projection | indexed edges | extraction/modeling gaps | observed decisions | observed conflicts | last observed domain |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| lock union/dev | 12912 | 3982 | 18000 | 9392 | `derive_more@2` |

The stopped invocation was the normal evidence command in release mode:

```text
cargo run --release -p rodin-core --bin rodin-cargo-evidence -- --append docs/design/rodin-c-evidence.md --json-out output/rodin-cargo-evidence.json
```

It was interrupted after the lock-union solve continued progressing through high-conflict domains without producing the matrix artifact. No Round 8 convergence table is claimed from partial output. The STOP is therefore specific: fixing the extraction bugs exposed a faithful lock-union search case that current Rodin does not complete promptly enough for the evidence matrix.

Verification completed for the code path changes:

| command | result |
|---|---|
| `cargo nextest run -p rodin-core -p rodin-facts` | 34/34 passed |
| `cargo clippy -p rodin-core -p rodin-facts -- -D warnings` | passed |

## Solver performance diagnosis: lock-union search pathology

Round 8 exposed a faithful lock-union/dev solve case with 12,912 indexed edges that did not complete promptly. This pass added feature-gated solver attribution under `rodin-core/perf-diagnostics` only; no decision-ordering, restart, learning, or extraction behavior changed.

The diagnostic invocation was:

```text
RODIN_DIAG_EVERY=1000 RODIN_DIAG_DECISION_LIMIT=18000 RUST_LOG=info,rodin_perf_diag=info stax record -- ./target/release/rodin-cargo-evidence
```

The run was stopped after the last emitted checkpoint at 13,000 decisions because profiler interval volume made progress past that checkpoint too slow for a useful 18,000-row sample. The earlier Round 8 uncapped stop remains the source for the observed 18,000 decisions / 9,392 conflicts / `derive_more@2` row; this diagnostic row is partial and is not extrapolated.

Conflict attribution was even narrower:

| class | top rows |
|---|---|
| decision package for conflict | `digest@0.11`: 6,778 |
| support edges | `anstream@1->proptest@1`, `anstyle-parse@1->proptest@1`, `camino@1->proptest@1`, `compact_str@0.9->proptest@1`, `console@0.15->proptest@1`, `const-oid@0.10->proptest@1`, `ctutils@0.4->proptest@1`, `dibs@0.2->proptest@1`: 6,777 each |
| learned-region duplicates | 1 duplicated region, repeated 6,777 times |

The repeated learned region is a large multi-domain region centered on `proptest@1 [1.9.0,1.9.1)` plus many support parents (`anstream@1`, `anstyle-parse@1`, `camino@1`, `compact_str@0.9`, `console@0.15`, `const-oid@0.10`, `ctutils@0.4`, `dibs@0.2`, `facet-json@0.50`, `facet-reflect@0.50`, `phon-engine@0.2`, `styx-format@5`, `toml_edit@0.22`, `winnow@0.5`, and others). Widening fires on every learned fact in this sample, but the widened fact still carries enough package support that it almost never becomes unit early: 6,778 learned facts produced 113,020,458 learned-fact scans and only 3,390 pruning events by 13k.

The implementation hot path matches the attribution. The live `stax` samples during the run put most active time under `rodin_core::solve` / `Solver::search`, with `Solver::propagate` and learned-fact handling dominating. Leaf samples were led by `Solver::propagate`, allocation/free/memmove, `String::clone`, `BTreeMap::clone`, `Vec<Interval>::clone`, and `EdgeStabilityWitness` clone/drop. The directly visible code path clones the full learned-fact vector on every exclusion pass, then scans every fact:

```text
fn apply_exclusions(&mut self, state: &mut State) -> Result<(), Conflict> {
    let learned = self.learned.clone();
    for (ix, fact) in learned.iter().enumerate() {
        ...
    }
}
```

and the search strategy chooses the lexicographically earliest active unresolved domain with more than one candidate:

```text
.filter(|(_, domain)| domain.active && domain.selected.is_none())
.filter(|(pkg, _)| self.candidates(state, **pkg).len() > 1)
.min_by_key(|(pkg, _)| self.index.name(**pkg).unwrap_or_default().to_owned())
```

That explains the geometric decision split (`digest` about half the decisions, `derive_more` about a quarter, `data-encoding` about an eighth): the solver is depth-first over a stable lexical domain order, trying candidates high-to-low, without activity-based variable selection or restarts.

Diagnosis:

- Primary class: decision heuristic / restart policy. The pathological branch is not random package fanout; it is deterministic lexical depth-first search walking a broad decision tree while conflicts repeatedly identify the same late support shape.
- Secondary class: learning-rate / learned-region usefulness. Learned facts are being minted and widened, but they are too support-wide to prune before the same branch shape is rediscovered. Duplicate-region suppression or subsumption would be a mechanical memory/time fix, but not a complete search fix.
- Mechanical implementation cost: learned-fact propagation should stop cloning the entire learned vector on every pass, and proof/witness payloads should be separated from the compact runtime exclusion data when scanning. This is a direct CPU/allocation reduction and does not decide solver strategy.
- Extraction over-width is not the primary explanation for this trace. The lock-union graph is large, but the attribution narrows to repeated `proptest@1` support through a small set of parents plus lexical decision order.

No search heuristic, restart policy, or learned-region semantic change was implemented in this pass.

| `cargo clippy -p rodin-core -p rodin-facts -- -D warnings` | passed |
| `cargo clippy -p rodin-core --features perf-diagnostics -- -D warnings` | passed |

## Solver performance fixes: lock-union completion

The accepted diagnosis pointed at three separable fixes. This pass implemented them in order with a hard invariant: the fixes may change search order and pruning cost, but not the selected set for a given problem. The invariant is covered by production-path tests that compare the legacy solver order against the fixed solver on Round 3 pressure scenarios, and compare lexical versus activity/restart search on a cargo-artifact extraction fixture.

Implemented changes:

- learned-fact propagation no longer clones the full learned vector before scanning;
- active learned facts are indexed by mentioned package, so propagation scans only facts touching active domains;
- learned-fact subsumption suppresses new facts contained by an active existing fact and deactivates active existing facts contained by a new broader fact;
- deactivated facts remain in the proof archive, so broader surviving facts can still replay through replaced exclusions;
- package activity is bumped from conflict attribution, decayed every conflict, and used to choose the next unresolved package; version candidates inside a package remain highest-first;
- geometric restarts clear the trail and keep learned facts.

Cumulative lock-union/dev measurements:

| level | status | decisions | conflicts | restarts | active learned facts | learned scans | prunes | subsumed | replaced | wall ms | note |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| before | did not complete | 18,000 | 9,392 | 0 | 6,778 at 13k checkpoint | 113,020,458 at 13k checkpoint | 3,390 at 13k checkpoint | 0 | 0 | - | Round 8/diagnosis stop; scan count is from the last diagnostic checkpoint, not extrapolated |
| fix1: indexed scan/no clone | stopped | 6,000 | 3,118 | 0 | 3,118 | 23,902,038 | 1,560 | 0 | 0 | - | same lexical search pathology; stopped after reproducing old shape |
| fix2: subsumption | capped | 18,038 | 9,392 | 0 | 2 | 92,398 | 4,696 | 9,390 | 0 | 161,124 | scan cost collapsed, search still did not complete before the 18k diagnostic cap |
| fix3: activity + restart | completed | 457 | 69 | 1 | 24 | 13,331 | 39 | 42 | 3 | 3,464 | lock-union/dev projection solved |

The fixed-target warm counterfactual dropped root dependency `copypatch`; warm/cold selected sets remained equal, with warm 235 decisions / 0 conflicts versus cold 238 decisions / 2 conflicts.

The remaining lock-oracle divergences are the already-classified model/extraction rows in the regenerated artifact; this performance pass did not change Cargo interpretation policy.

## Round 9: Per-row Cargo oracle verdict table

Source snapshot: `/Users/amos/oss/facet-cc` Cargo.lock plus local Cargo registry source/cache artifacts. The production extractor is offline-only, reads local Cargo.toml files and `.crate` archives directly, indexes cached registry alternatives for locked package names, and models Cargo-compatible coexisting domains keyed by package name and compatibility class. The fresh-lock oracle is test-only: it runs Cargo offline in a throwaway workspace copy and never feeds Rodin's solve path. Every divergence row below carries exactly one verdict and, for accepted divergences, exactly one policy rule.

### Fresh-lock oracle

| attempted | solved | lock packages | unique names | wall ms | message |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| true | true | 892 | 813 | 1108 | cargo generate-lockfile --offline succeeded in throwaway copy |

### Oracle matrix

| projection | oracle | exact matches | compared | match rate | divergences | decisions | conflicts | wall ms |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| fixed-target runtime | HistoricalLock | 790 | 892 | 88.57% | 128 | 238 | 2 | 1532 |
| fixed-target runtime | FreshLock | 795 | 892 | 89.13% | 122 | 238 | 2 | 1532 |
| lock-union dev-included | HistoricalLock | 848 | 892 | 95.07% | 111 | 457 | 69 | 3419 |
| lock-union dev-included | FreshLock | 853 | 892 | 95.63% | 105 | 457 | 69 | 3419 |

### Accepted divergences

| policy | Rodin rule |
|---|---|
| fixed-target inactive dev edge | The fixed-target runtime projection keeps dev-dependencies active only for workspace members; packages reachable only through non-workspace dev edges are outside that projection. |
| fixed-target inactive target edge | The fixed-target runtime projection evaluates target-conditional edges for aarch64-apple-darwin and does not select packages reachable only through inactive target predicates. |
| historical-lock superseded by fresh oracle | A historical Cargo.lock row is accepted when the fresh Cargo oracle selects the same package domain and version as Rodin. |
| lockfile-free graph membership | Rodin treats Cargo.lock as an oracle, not as a root set; a locked package class is absent when the extracted manifest closure does not require that domain. |
| lockfile-free version choice | Rodin does not preserve a historical or fresh Cargo.lock version when multiple cached versions satisfy the active ranges; it selects the highest version in the package domain under the canonical order. |
| manifest-closure extra selection | Rodin solves the extracted manifest closure over the offline cache, so it may select an internally justified cached package domain that Cargo's lockfile did not retain. |

### Projection: fixed-target runtime

Product-default projection: target-conditionals are evaluated against `aarch64-apple-darwin`, build dependencies are active for the host, and dev dependencies are active only for workspace members.

## Round 10 — R2 read-set stability demonstration

Round 10 is the explicit R2 crown-jewel demonstration: the same offline sparse-index fixture is solved twice, once with declared-structure widening and once with observed read-set widening. The fixture rows live under `crates/rodin-core/fixtures/r2-sparse-index/` and are parsed with `facet-json` through the `rodin-survey` sparse-index row types, so this round never hits the network.

| scenario | edge | declared versions pruned | read-set versions pruned | declared added prunes | read-set added prunes | read-set digest | snapshot |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| cosmetic-dependency-feature-diff | demo-app@1 -> demo-core@1 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 2 | `00e6deacf6201af11a16486943af4edd0fea2951f987d7e4ff89f5b2107ecb91` | `r2-fixture:a2791b8383c755469d39bd70d6ee8299604f22134e8742859146be9302f3134f` |

Consumed read-set fields:

- `deps[demo-core].actual`
- `deps[demo-core].kind`
- `deps[demo-core].optional`
- `deps[demo-core].req`

Stable versions certified by the observed read set: 1.0.0, 1.0.1, 1.0.2.

The middle `demo-app` fixture row differs cosmetically in dependency feature/default-feature metadata, so the existing `EdgeSignature` declared-structure lane cannot include it. The conflict's observed version-requirement read set does not consume those cosmetic fields, so read-set stability verifies the full three-version range.

## Round 11 — Real crates.io neighborhood R2 demonstration

Round 11 runs the same declared-structure versus observed-read-set widening comparison on a committed real sparse-index snapshot. The fixture rows live under `crates/rodin-core/fixtures/r2-real-sparse-index/`; tests and report generation parse those rows offline with the `rodin-survey` sparse-index row types and do not hit the network.

| source | scenario | neighborhood versions | declared versions pruned | read-set versions pruned | declared added prunes | read-set added prunes |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| synthetic Round 10 | cosmetic-dependency-feature-diff | 3 | 2 | 3 | 1 | 2 |
| real Round 11 | reqwest-0.13-hyper-target-cfg-diff | 5 | 2 | 5 | 1 | 4 |

Chosen neighborhood: `reqwest` versions 0.13.0, 0.13.1, 0.13.2, 0.13.3, 0.13.4. Edge: `reqwest@0.13 -> hyper@1`. Snapshot: `r2-real-fixture:9079e92712ee717965f051a5c8173dae5977eadeaa0214ca9c8f09e4bf2ae7e8`. Read-set digest: `7a91f329b86e411eecfd3373861474c5658b5fe1c253536fbbf607c8682b7ec7`.

Consumed read-set fields:

- `deps[hyper].actual`
- `deps[hyper].kind`
- `deps[hyper].optional`
- `deps[hyper].req`

Cosmetic fields that differed while the consumed set held:

- `deps[hyper].target`: `cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))` on reqwest 0.13.0-0.13.2, then `cfg(not(all(target_arch = "wasm32", any(target_os = "unknown", target_os = "none"))))` on 0.13.3-0.13.4
- `cksum`, `pubtime`, and `rust_version` differ between the committed sparse-index rows, but none are resolver-consumed by this conflict
- Several unrelated target-gated dependency rows change at the same reqwest 0.13.3 boundary; the observed support edge remains `reqwest@0.13 -> hyper@1`

Declared-structure stable versions: 0.13.3, 0.13.4.

Observed-read-set stable versions: 0.13.0, 0.13.1, 0.13.2, 0.13.3, 0.13.4.

The real-data gap is smaller than an idealized all-cosmetic patch run but still fires on committed crates.io rows: declared `EdgeSignature` equality stops at the target-cfg split, while the conflict's observed read set only consumes `actual`, `kind`, `optional`, and `req`.
